## 2. Evidence basis and inspection method

Evidence was derived from connected GitHub repository metadata, the final-tree snapshot, selected source/notebook/README contents, and commit-history records. The analysis prioritizes implementation and explicit provenance markers over names or aspirations.

The inspection hierarchy is: (1) source/config/notebook contents; (2) explicit course/platform/generated markers; (3) final-tree structure and stored outputs; (4) commit chronology; and only then bounded inference. This order matters in this batch because several repositories have ambitious technical titles but no substantive content, while others contain substantial guided coursework whose framework must not be credited as original authorship.

Evidence interpretation:

- count only the directly visible implementation or executed-learning signal;
- preserve course/platform/generated provenance beside the claim;
- treat absent evidence as absent rather than filling gaps from the repository name;
- do not convert local experimentation into production ownership.

# Repository 054 / 134 — `NLP-with-classification-and-vector-spaces`

## Project identity

**Descriptive name:** **Guided NLP Sentiment Classification Practice**

A compact, course-derived NLP practice repository containing executed sentiment-analysis notebooks for logistic regression and Naive Bayes using NLTK Twitter samples. The notebooks expose train/test splitting, tweet preprocessing, feature-frequency construction and probabilistic classification while retaining explicit graded-assignment markers and helper utilities that must not be treated as independently authored framework code.

# Repository 056 / 134 — `NLP-with-probabilistic-models`

## Project identity

**Descriptive name:** **Guided Probabilistic NLP / HMM / Language-Model Practice**

A probabilistic NLP study repository combining detailed notes on n-gram language modeling and smoothing with a guided POS-tagging notebook based on Hidden Markov Models and Wall Street Journal corpora. Explicit graded-function markers and helper modules identify course scaffolding; the repository nevertheless demonstrates hands-on work with vocabulary construction, unknown-word handling, transition/emission statistics and probabilistic sequence modeling.

# Repository 057 / 134 — `Security`

## Project identity

**Descriptive name:** **Early Password-Transformation Security Experiment**

A small private Python experiment that hashes input with SHA-256, uses the digest as a deterministic seed for Python’s pseudorandom generator, and emits a short mixed-character string. It demonstrates exploration of hashing and deterministic generation, but it is not a secure password-storage design and should be retained primarily as evidence of an early security misconception and the engineering lessons that follow from it.

Correct classification:

> **A small private Python experiment that hashes input with SHA-256, uses the digest as a deterministic seed for Python’s pseudorandom generator, and emits a short mixed-character string. It demonstrates exploration of hashing and deterministic generation, but it is not a secure password-storage design and should be retained primarily as evidence of an early security misconception and the engineering lessons that follow from it.**

## 4. Core technical scope

A small private Python experiment that hashes input with SHA-256, uses the digest as a deterministic seed for Python’s pseudorandom generator, and emits a short mixed-character string. It demonstrates exploration of hashing and deterministic generation, but it is not a secure password-storage design and should be retained primarily as evidence of an early security misconception and the engineering lessons that follow from it.

Directly evidenced scope:

- Python `hashlib` SHA-256 use
- deterministic PRNG seeding
- character-set generation
- basic password/security experimentation

Evidence interpretation:

- count only the directly visible implementation or executed-learning signal;
- preserve course/platform/generated provenance beside the claim;
- treat absent evidence as absent rather than filling gaps from the repository name;
- do not convert local experimentation into production ownership.

- three-commit sequence: initialize, create file, rename with `.py` extension

---

## 6. Algorithm reconstruction

The function computes SHA-256 over input text, converts the digest to an integer, seeds Python’s general-purpose PRNG with that value, and selects a fixed number of characters from an alphanumeric/symbol alphabet. The output is deterministic for the same input. This is a custom encoding/transformation experiment, not a standard password-hashing construction.

Evidence interpretation:

- count only the directly visible implementation or executed-learning signal;
- preserve course/platform/generated provenance beside the claim;
- treat absent evidence as absent rather than filling gaps from the repository name;
- do not convert local experimentation into production ownership.

---

## 7. Why this is not password hashing

Password storage requires deliberately expensive, salted password KDFs such as Argon2, scrypt, bcrypt or appropriately configured PBKDF2. A fast SHA-256 digest followed by deterministic PRNG output remains cheap to evaluate and lacks per-record salt/work-factor semantics. Calling the result a password hash would therefore be misleading.

Evidence interpretation:

- count only the directly visible implementation or executed-learning signal;
- preserve course/platform/generated provenance beside the claim;
- treat absent evidence as absent rather than filling gaps from the repository name;
- do not convert local experimentation into production ownership.

---

## 8. PRNG and cryptographic-boundary lesson

Python’s `random` module is designed for simulation/general pseudorandomness, not security-sensitive secrets. Seeding it deterministically from a digest makes repeatability the feature. This repository is useful for distinguishing “looks random” from “has a cryptographic security property.”

Evidence interpretation:

- count only the directly visible implementation or executed-learning signal;
- preserve course/platform/generated provenance beside the claim;
- treat absent evidence as absent rather than filling gaps from the repository name;
- do not convert local experimentation into production ownership.

## 10. Secure redesign path

A production redesign would not patch this custom algorithm. It would replace it with a vetted authentication/password library using a standard KDF, random salt, encoded parameters, constant-time verification where applicable, secret-management boundaries and tests around registration, verification and migration behavior.

Evidence interpretation:

# Repository 058 / 134 — `Video-Tools`

## Project identity

**Descriptive name:** **OpenCV Video-to-Frame Extraction Utility**

A single-purpose Python/OpenCV utility that opens an input video, reads frame-rate/count metadata, computes duration, creates an output directory, writes every decoded frame to sequential JPEG files and releases the capture. It is a small but concrete authored tool with clear I/O behavior and straightforward opportunities for robustness and CLI evolution.

Correct classification:

> **A single-purpose Python/OpenCV utility that opens an input video, reads frame-rate/count metadata, computes duration, creates an output directory, writes every decoded frame to sequential JPEG files and releases the capture. It is a small but concrete authored tool with clear I/O behavior and straightforward opportunities for robustness and CLI evolution.**

## 6. Video metadata handling

The script queries original FPS and total frame count, then derives a duration. This shows awareness that media files carry timing metadata that should be surfaced before processing. The missing guard for zero or invalid FPS is the main robustness gap in that calculation.

Evidence interpretation:

- count only the directly visible implementation or executed-learning signal;
- preserve course/platform/generated provenance beside the claim;
- treat absent evidence as absent rather than filling gaps from the repository name;
- do not convert local experimentation into production ownership.

---

## 7. Frame extraction loop

The implementation uses the standard `read()` loop until decoding fails/end-of-stream, then writes frames with zero-padded sequential names. This is simple and understandable, making the dataflow easy to audit: video stream → decoded frame → filesystem JPEG.

Evidence interpretation:

- count only the directly visible implementation or executed-learning signal;
- preserve course/platform/generated provenance beside the claim;
- treat absent evidence as absent rather than filling gaps from the repository name;
- do not convert local experimentation into production ownership.

---

## 8. 30-FPS comment mismatch

The leading comment says the function extracts “30 frames per second,” but there is no temporal resampling logic. Every source frame is saved. If the input is 24, 30, 60 or variable FPS, the output count follows decoded frames, not a fixed 30-Hz sampling policy. This is a documentation/behavior mismatch worth preserving as an engineering lesson.

Evidence interpretation:

- count only the directly visible implementation or executed-learning signal;
- preserve course/platform/generated provenance beside the claim;
- treat absent evidence as absent rather than filling gaps from the repository name;
- do not convert local experimentation into production ownership.

---

## 9. Storage and throughput boundary

Saving every frame can multiply a compressed video into thousands of JPEG files and substantial disk usage. A production data-preparation tool would expose interval/FPS sampling, image quality, output format, maximum-frame and overwrite controls, and would report failures without printing one line per frame at very large scale.

Evidence interpretation:

- count only the directly visible implementation or executed-learning signal;
- preserve course/platform/generated provenance beside the claim;
- treat absent evidence as absent rather than filling gaps from the repository name;
- do not convert local experimentation into production ownership.

---

The cleanest next step would be a parameterized CLI: input path, output directory, sampling rate, naming pattern and image quality, plus validation and tests. That would turn a hard-coded personal script into a repeatable tool without changing the core OpenCV loop.

Evidence interpretation:

- count only the directly visible implementation or executed-learning signal;
- preserve course/platform/generated provenance beside the claim;

# Repository 062 / 134 — `RDBMS-For-Data-Engineering`

## Project identity

**Descriptive name:** **Guided RDBMS Normalization and Constraint Practice**

A compact relational-database practice repository focused on DDL, constraints, primary keys, decomposition/normalization and basic Datasette use. The BookShop exercises move repeated author data into a separate author-details table and experiment with key constraints. Several filenames overstate the final implementation—`FK.sql` does not visibly define a foreign key, and the Stored Procedures directory is empty—so direct evidence is narrower than topic labels.

Correct classification:

> **A compact relational-database practice repository focused on DDL, constraints, primary keys, decomposition/normalization and basic Datasette use. The BookShop exercises move repeated author data into a separate author-details table and experiment with key constraints. Several filenames overstate the final implementation—`FK.sql` does not visibly define a foreign key, and the Stored Procedures directory is empty—so direct evidence is narrower than topic labels.**

## 40. What production evolution would require

A production evolution would require: (1) a stable packaged implementation; (2) dependency/environment pinning; (3) input/schema validation; (4) automated unit/integration tests; (5) logging/metrics and explicit error semantics; (6) secure secrets/data handling where applicable; (7) repeatable deployment and rollback; and (8) documentation sufficient for another engineer to reproduce the system.

For this repository specifically, the starting point would be to turn **IBM Cloud Db2 service provisioning** into a concrete, versioned capability rather than expanding the title.

Evidence interpretation:

- count only the directly visible implementation or executed-learning signal;
- preserve course/platform/generated provenance beside the claim;
- treat absent evidence as absent rather than filling gaps from the repository name;
- do not convert local experimentation into production ownership.
